# Coastwatch against the issue-293 fleet-auth (AC16)

coastwatch commit:  04f52493340fdffda8f5cd9447d89a6bead8b8ca  (HEAD of /home/jakub/code/coastwatch)
trawl commit:       672993d2202e01aa1b2e0d3829d79e1710393a2a  (branch fix/issue-293-server-max-concurrent-requests-becomes)
                    contains 672993d2 fix(fleet-auth): run at most four argon2 checks at once
                    crates/fleet-auth and crates/fleet-ui had no uncommitted changes during the run
toolchain:          rustc 1.95.0 (59807616e 2026-04-14), cargo-nextest 0.9.143 (coastwatch rust-toolchain.toml)
run (UTC):          2026-10-09T01:28:38Z .. 2026-10-09T01:30:51Z

## Layout

A scratch sibling layout, so coastwatch's path dependency
`fleet-auth = { path = "../trawl/crates/fleet-auth" }` resolves to this branch:

  .flow-scratch/cw/coastwatch-trawl-293/   the coastwatch tree at 04f52493, byte-for-byte
                                           (1011 entries checked against the commit's blob hashes;
                                           exported with git ls-tree + git cat-file, not a git checkout)
  .flow-scratch/cw/trawl -> the issue-293 trawl worktree
  CARGO_TARGET_DIR=.flow-scratch/cw/target

The coastwatch directory is not named plain `coastwatch`. coastwatch-env derives the
test database from the checkout directory name, so this run used its own
`coastwatch_test_trawl_293` and never the canonical `coastwatch_test`.

fleet-ui 0.9.2 /home/jakub/code/trawl/.flow-worktrees/trawl-issue-293-server-max-concurrent-requests-becomes/.flow-scratch/cw/trawl/crates/fleet-ui/Cargo.toml

Cargo updated only the two path packages in coastwatch's Cargo.lock. The result is
byte-identical to the uncommitted Cargo.lock in the main coastwatch checkout. No
registry package changed. The added "serde" line is fleet-ui's dependency list,
naming a package the lockfile already pins:

## Command

coastwatch documents `cargo nextest run --workspace` as its test command (AGENTS.md,
README "run tests"). Its harness hard-fails integration tests under plain `cargo test`,
because they share a process-wide PgPool. So the suite ran under nextest, with the
test-tier environment that AGENTS.md and the pre-push hook resolve. Doc tests, which
nextest does not run, ran under `cargo test --doc`. Together they cover the tests
`cargo test --workspace` would select.

  cd .flow-scratch/cw/coastwatch-trawl-293
  export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<worktree>/.flow-scratch/cw/target
  export COASTWATCH_ENV=test; set -a; eval "$(cargo run -q -p coastwatch-config --bin coastwatch-env)"; set +a
  cargo nextest run --workspace --no-fail-fast
  cargo test --workspace --doc -j 4

Test-tier databases (credentials are the plaintext test stubs in config/coastwatch.toml):
  DATABASE_URL       postgres://coastwatch_test:***@127.0.0.1:5432/coastwatch_test_trawl_293
  FLEET_DATABASE_URL postgres://fleet_test:***@127.0.0.1:5432/fleet_test
  server: the coastwatch-test-db-postgres-1 container (test tier)

## Earlier cold-database attempt

Before the run below, partition 1 of 4 ran against the newly created
coastwatch_test_trawl_293. 15 of its 793 tests failed, all at the same place while
several test processes migrated the new database at once. The failures are in
coastwatch's test bootstrap, not in fleet-auth. Afterwards the database held all 66
migrations, each with success = true, and every test passed in the full run below.
